# Phase 1 and Phase 2 — concept-by-concept learning resources

Updated 20 October 2026, Africa/Kampala. **Selected-video audit completed with documented reading fallbacks. This is not a video-only course.** This is the one table-based guide for both phases.

## Read this first

- All 246 required module concepts are covered in 248 study rows. Two preserved supporting rows expand object lifetime/garbage collection and dependency management. No required concept is omitted.
- Videos are preferred. A video title or uninspected candidate is never presented as verified teaching.
- **225/248 entries have partial reviewed video support; 23 do not.** Partial support does not complete a concept.
- Reviewed audiovisual source material totals 365.6 unique source minutes (overlapping ranges counted once) across 62 of 67 candidate sources. Audio was acquired/transcribed; only sparse frames were inspected. This is command-line-assisted research, not proof of autonomous agent operation.
- **248/248 selected reading/practice paths have passed the source-depth and practice-mapping audit.** Remaining reading-path audits: 0. This is separate from video completeness and learner mastery.
- For each concept: use the video where available, read the linked required source sections for gaps, then do the module practice. Exercise codes are module assignments, not a claim that every exercise tests every row.
- Watch each shared segment once and complete each shared exercise once. Several concept rows deliberately point to the same material; revisit only the part needed for a remaining gap.
- All learner completion remains Not assessed. Use this as a mixed video/reading/practice resource package; it does not certify learner mastery or complete video coverage.

## What validated means

A valid learning-resource path must match the pinned concept/depth, identify the selected teaching sections, retain corrections/version limits and map to practice or an explicit assessment check. Video support here requires review of ASR transcripts produced from acquired actual audio plus manually inspected sampled frames; it does not mean a human listened to the audio or watched the full video. A title, caption or successful URL check is insufficient. Resource validation is separate from your mastery. The retained-candidate/excerpt audit has a documented disposition for every source. Complete video-only coverage is not certified. A validated reading/practice path does not certify a complete video lesson or learner mastery.

## Study order

Phase1: Python → NumPy → mathematics → PyTorch → neural networks → transformers → GPU/memory. Then Phase1 assessment. Phase2: request lifecycle → prefill/decode/KV → memory/quantization → batching/scheduling → metrics → profiling → serving. Repository estimates 90h + 60h include assessments.

## Concept tables

Direct reading links are suggested starting pages selected from the module source pack; the Required scope link retains the authoritative section list. The selected concept scopes have been independently reviewed; full pages and the additional source-pack collections retain their stated study requirements. A video link includes its inspected time range when the mapping is exact; otherwise it opens the source’s reviewed-range table below. The scope column says exactly what was mapped to this concept. Unmapped concepts receive reading fallback and an explicit pending-video label, not an unrelated recommendation.

### phase-1 / M01 — prerequisites and outcomes

[Module instructions](<https://github.com/noduxa/ai-inference-engineering/blob/0323221d26075752fcf59a18c5752e608786fc98/phase-1/modules/01-advanced-python.md>)

**Prerequisites:** Existing programming experience, basic Python syntax and command-line use. No AI specialization is assumed.

**Required outcomes:**

- Predict aliasing, lifetime and lazy-evaluation behaviour in short Python
  programs.
- Build a tested package with typed boundaries and reliable cleanup.
- Choose a concurrency mechanism from a workload description and measure its
  overhead.
- Locate an allocation or CPU hotspot and trace a public repository call path.

| phase-1-M01-C03 | Object lifetime and garbage collection (supporting detail) | [V63 2:40–4:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iHVs_HkjdmI&t=160); [V63 6:00–8:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iHVs_HkjdmI&t=360); [V67 3:30–5:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rQQ3yStAOGQ&t=210) | CPython expert explains increment/decrement reference counts and deallocation when counts reach zero, including a cascade when a dying object releases another object. Historical2014 conceptual model for conventionalCPython. Cycles, finalizer resurrection, immortal objects and modern free-threaded/deferred accounting require separate limits; Python language semantics do not guarantee exact finalization timing. A binding does not always allocate a fresh object.; Explains why mutual cycles can retain positive reference counts after external references vanish, and distinguishes periodic cyclic collection from zero-count deallocation. Historical conceptual simplification: GC tracks eligible containers and uses temporary bookkeeping to subtract internal references, not blindly mutating all live object reference counts or scanning every object on every collection. Exact generations/thresholds vary byCPython version/build. Reachable old objects are not garbage merely because of age. No manualGC tuning or disabling performed.; OfficialPyTorch demonstration shows a categorized GPU allocation timeline across training iterations, then a reference-cycle example retainingGPU tensors until cyclic collection. Historical2023 training visualization, not inference memory accounting or reserved-versus-allocated API measurement. Gradients/optimizer states are not ordinary no-grad inference requirements. zero_grad(set_to_none=True) addresses the earlier specific example, not all leaks. gc.collect every100iterations is illustrative, not a default fix; remove unintended cycles and investigate ownership. Current profiler APIs/options must be checked. No localGPU workload executed. Reading review: Data model lifetime/finalization contract reviewed; bounded cyclic-object check confirms explicit CPython collection without assuming portable timing. Independent check: Explain unreachable cycles, retained references and explicit resource cleanup; contrast gc/tracemalloc/RSS measurements. | [Data model](https://docs.python.org/3/reference/datamodel.html) — Data model §3.1 garbage collection and external resources; CPython reference counting is an implementation detail, immediate finalization is not portable.; [Required scope](https://github.com/noduxa/ai-inference-engineering/blob/0323221d26075752fcf59a18c5752e608786fc98/phase-1/notebooklm/source-packs/01-advanced-python.md) | Targeted check with [PY-05](<https://github.com/noduxa/ai-inference-engineering/blob/0323221d26075752fcf59a18c5752e608786fc98/phase-1/exercises/01-advanced-python.md>) |

### phase-1 / M02 — prerequisites and outcomes

[Module instructions](<https://github.com/noduxa/ai-inference-engineering/blob/0323221d26075752fcf59a18c5752e608786fc98/phase-1/modules/02-numpy-and-tensors.md>)

**Prerequisites:** Complete the preceding module’s core shape, calculation or programming checks; revisit any unresolved prerequisite before continuing.

**Required outcomes:**

- Predict result shapes before executing array operations.
- Explain aliasing and layout using strides, views and contiguous storage.
- Estimate payload bytes and compare dtypes without assuming equal numerical
  behaviour.
- Measure a controlled loop/vectorized comparison and discuss temporary
  allocations.

### phase-1 / M03 — prerequisites and outcomes

[Module instructions](<https://github.com/noduxa/ai-inference-engineering/blob/0323221d26075752fcf59a18c5752e608786fc98/phase-1/modules/03-essential-mathematics.md>)

**Prerequisites:** Complete the preceding module’s core shape, calculation or programming checks; revisit any unresolved prerequisite before continuing.

**Required outcomes:**

- Calculate small matrix products, tensor shapes, gradients and stable softmax
  values.
- Connect linear transformations and probability notation to tensor programs.
- Recognize rank, basis, orthogonality, eigenvectors and SVD without proof-heavy
  derivations.
- Explain numerical failure modes using finite-precision reasoning.

### phase-1 / M04 — prerequisites and outcomes

[Module instructions](<https://github.com/noduxa/ai-inference-engineering/blob/0323221d26075752fcf59a18c5752e608786fc98/phase-1/modules/04-pytorch-fundamentals.md>)

**Prerequisites:** Complete the preceding module’s core shape, calculation or programming checks; revisit any unresolved prerequisite before continuing.

**Required outcomes:**

- Build and inspect a small module with named parameters and a reproducible
  forward pass.
- Perform one training step and distinguish parameter updates from forward-only
  inference.
- Save/reload a state dictionary and verify equivalent outputs.
- Measure timing and memory using correct device-specific boundaries.

FLOPs/byte is arithmetic intensity. Input/product/accumulator/output dtypes are hardware/kernel dependent; not all operations benefit or remain accurate. No RTX3050 mixed-precision benchmark or complete AMP recipe. Reading review: AMP op-specific autocast, gradient-scaling role and device/dtype caveats reviewed. ### phase-1 / M05 — prerequisites and outcomes

[Module instructions](<https://github.com/noduxa/ai-inference-engineering/blob/0323221d26075752fcf59a18c5752e608786fc98/phase-1/modules/05-neural-networks.md>)

**Prerequisites:** Complete the preceding module’s core shape, calculation or programming checks; revisit any unresolved prerequisite before continuing.

**Required outcomes:**

- Explain what parameters learn in regression, classification and an MLP.
- Trace forward propagation, loss, backward propagation and an update.
- Investigate one learning-rate or regularization change under controlled
  conditions.
- Explain generalization and the work omitted during inference.

### phase-1 / M06 — prerequisites and outcomes

[Module instructions](<https://github.com/noduxa/ai-inference-engineering/blob/0323221d26075752fcf59a18c5752e608786fc98/phase-1/modules/06-transformers.md>)

**Prerequisites:** Complete the preceding module’s core shape, calculation or programming checks; revisit any unresolved prerequisite before continuing.

**Required outcomes:**

- Trace text through tokens, embeddings, transformer blocks and output logits.
- Calculate tiny masked attention and annotate every intermediate shape.
- Distinguish encoder, decoder, encoder-decoder and decoder-only models.
- Explain and inspect one-token-at-a-time generation and sampling controls.

| ID | Required concept | Reviewed video support | Scope / still needed | Required reading | Practice |

| phase-1-M06-C01 | Tokenization and vocabulary | [V14 3:03–5:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wjZofJX0v4M&t=183) | Token/vocabulary overview, not a tokenizer implementation. Reading review: A request converts text to IDs, embeds and adds positional information, applies causal blocks, projects to last-position logits, selects/appends a token and checks stopping. No pretrained weights required. | [Transformer architectures](https://huggingface.co/learn/llm-course/chapter1/6) — Decoder-only generation: sequential conditional prediction; [Generation configuration](https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/main_classes/text_generation) — do_sample, temperature, top_k, top_p and max_new_tokens; [Required scope](https://github.com/noduxa/ai-inference-engineering/blob/0323221d26075752fcf59a18c5752e608786fc98/phase-1/notebooklm/source-packs/06-transformers.md) | Targeted check with [TR-03](<https://github.com/noduxa/ai-inference-engineering/blob/0323221d26075752fcf59a18c5752e608786fc98/phase-1/exercises/06-transformers.md>) |

### phase-1 / M07 — prerequisites and outcomes

[Module instructions](<https://github.com/noduxa/ai-inference-engineering/blob/0323221d26075752fcf59a18c5752e608786fc98/phase-1/modules/07-gpu-architecture-and-memory.md>)

**Prerequisites:** Complete the preceding module’s core shape, calculation or programming checks; revisit any unresolved prerequisite before continuing.

**Required outcomes:**

- Draw a conceptual execution and memory map for a CPU/GPU system.
- Estimate tensor payload memory across precision formats and state overhead
  limits.
- Interpret utilization, allocated memory and reserved memory without conflating
  them.
- Design a bounded resource experiment and diagnose likely memory or compute
  limits.

### phase-2 / P2-M01 — prerequisites and outcomes

[Module instructions](<https://github.com/noduxa/ai-inference-engineering/blob/0323221d26075752fcf59a18c5752e608786fc98/phase-2/modules/01-inference-request-lifecycle.md>)

**Prerequisites:** Pass the Phase 1 evidence gate: shapes, autograd, attention, dtype and memory reasoning.

**Required outcomes:**

- Trace text through configuration, weights, tokenization, placement, forward
  pass and streamed output.
- Identify logits processing, token selection, stop conditions and decoding.
- Separate model loading, first request and warmed execution.

### phase-2 / P2-M01

| ID | Required concept | Reviewed video support | Scope / still needed | Required reading | Practice |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| phase-2-P2-M01-C01 | Configuration | [V56 0:00–2:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AhChOFRegn4&t=0); [V56 2:00–3:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AhChOFRegn4&t=120) | Separates configuration, model architecture/class, random initialization and loading pretrained weights; outlines AutoModel.from_pretrained from a Hub checkpoint or local folder. Historical conceptual API overview; supported model classes and compatible trusted artifacts are required, not arbitrary checkpoints. This is model construction/loading only, not server readiness/unloading or a file-format comparison. ASR Inkface app means Hugging Face Hub, PART meansBART. No model weights downloaded or executed.; Shows constructing randomly initialized BERT from a config versus loading pretrained parameters; demonstrates changing layer count and save_pretrained/from_pretrained paths. Changing architecture can invalidate checkpoint weight shapes; do not assume pretrained compatibility. Random construction is not training; initialization distribution/control remains reading. ### phase-2 / P2-M02 — prerequisites and outcomes

[Module instructions](<https://github.com/noduxa/ai-inference-engineering/blob/0323221d26075752fcf59a18c5752e608786fc98/phase-2/modules/02-prefill-decode-and-kv-cache.md>)

**Prerequisites:** Complete the preceding module’s evidence and exit test; retain its assumptions for comparisons.

**Required outcomes:**

- Explain the different work done during prefill and decode.
- Estimate cache growth using layer, KV-head, sequence, batch and dtype
  assumptions.
- Distinguish logical context, allocated cache capacity, prefix reuse and
  eviction.

### phase-2 / P2-M02

| ID | Required concept | Reviewed video support | Scope / still needed | Required reading | Practice |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| phase-2-P2-M02-C01 | Autoregression | [V30 1:06:04–1:08:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Q1HOKrNeh2M&t=3964) | Next-token autoregression intuition only; implementation remains.; Predict, append to context and repeat. Word-level illustration only; tokenization, KV state, stopping and causal mask implementation still need separate study. Reading review: Cache explanation and pinned lab autoregressive forward/select/append loop reviewed. Independent check: Trace conditional next-token predictions and explain why future sampled tokens require successive forward calls. | [How caching works](https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/cache_explanation) — Cache explanation attention and iterative generation; E01 next-token loop. | phase-2-P2-M02-C02 | prefill | [V17 22:34–24:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=EfM546A79aM&t=1354); [V17 21:40–23:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=EfM546A79aM&t=1300); [V17 30:30–32:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=EfM546A79aM&t=1830) | Prompt prefill populates KV cache; implementation and measurements remain.; Causal-prefix reuse and prompt prefill populating K/V state. Decode steps, memory capacity and cross-request prefix matching require separate study.; Analytical example, not actual hardware timings. S*T/(S+T), S/2 and S/(S+1) rely on this simplified attention/byte-traffic model; H100 balance threshold is hardware-specific. ### phase-2 / P2-M03 — prerequisites and outcomes

[Module instructions](<https://github.com/noduxa/ai-inference-engineering/blob/0323221d26075752fcf59a18c5752e608786fc98/phase-2/modules/03-model-memory-and-quantization.md>)

**Prerequisites:** Complete the preceding module’s evidence and exit test; retain its assumptions for comparisons.

**Required outcomes:**

- Build a memory budget including weights, activations, KV state, workspace and
  overhead.
- Compare two supported precisions and interpret memory, timing and output
  differences.
- Diagnose an OOM without treating reserved memory as a leak.

### phase-2 / P2-M04 — prerequisites and outcomes

[Module instructions](<https://github.com/noduxa/ai-inference-engineering/blob/0323221d26075752fcf59a18c5752e608786fc98/phase-2/modules/04-batching-scheduling-and-serving.md>)

**Prerequisites:** Complete the preceding module’s evidence and exit test; retain its assumptions for comparisons.

**Required outcomes:**

- Explain static, dynamic and continuous batching with a request timeline.
- Measure concurrency and batch-size effects on throughput and individual
  latency.
- Specify bounded admission, cancellation and backpressure behavior.

### phase-2 / P2-M04

### phase-2 / P2-M05 — prerequisites and outcomes

[Module instructions](<https://github.com/noduxa/ai-inference-engineering/blob/0323221d26075752fcf59a18c5752e608786fc98/phase-2/modules/05-performance-metrics-and-benchmarking.md>)

**Prerequisites:** Complete the preceding module’s evidence and exit test; retain its assumptions for comparisons.

**Required outcomes:**

- Define TTFT, TPOT, ITL and throughput with explicit clocks and token counts.
- Design a repeated, controlled comparison with raw per-request records.
- Explain when tail percentiles and cross-hardware comparisons are misleading.

### phase-2 / P2-M06 — prerequisites and outcomes

[Module instructions](<https://github.com/noduxa/ai-inference-engineering/blob/0323221d26075752fcf59a18c5752e608786fc98/phase-2/modules/06-profiling-and-bottleneck-diagnosis.md>)

**Prerequisites:** Complete the preceding module’s evidence and exit test; retain its assumptions for comparisons.

**Required outcomes:**

- Use CPU and device observations to form a falsifiable performance hypothesis.
- Read a profiler timeline and distinguish activity from causal evidence.
- Change one variable and report uncertainty and failed hypotheses.

### phase-2 / P2-M06

| ID | Required concept | Reviewed video support | Scope / still needed | Required reading | Practice |

| phase-2-P2-M06-C01 | CPU | [V49 22:40–24:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jmzIvQZCLZM&t=1360) | The V1 talk motivates reducing Python scheduler overhead and separating CPU scheduling from GPU execution. Historical March2025 synchronous-scheduling claim, not current vLLM behavior. Lines of code is a rough complexity proxy, not latency evidence. Near-zero overhead is a design goal; no local speedup established. Reading review: CPU-side tokenization, dispatch, serialization and Python work can delay device input; cProfile internal and cumulative time attribute different boundaries and do not establish native-kernel efficiency. ### phase-2 / P2-M07 — prerequisites and outcomes

[Module instructions](<https://github.com/noduxa/ai-inference-engineering/blob/0323221d26075752fcf59a18c5752e608786fc98/phase-2/modules/07-serving-systems-and-vllm-bridge.md>)

**Prerequisites:** Complete the preceding module’s evidence and exit test; retain its assumptions for comparisons.

**Required outcomes:**

- Map API, scheduling, execution, cache and streaming responsibilities.
- Explain PagedAttention conceptually without claiming kernel expertise.
- Prepare a code-reading and development plan for Phase 3 with evidence gaps.

## Required reading tables by module

These are the original curriculum source-pack selections, not a claim that each current webpage was independently re-reviewed in this session. Read only the sections needed for your concept/exercise.

### phase-1 / 01-advanced-python.md

| Reading | Selected sections / scope |
|---|---|
| [Data model](https://docs.python.org/3/reference/datamodel.html) | Objects, values and types; standard type hierarchy; callable types; context managers |
| [Python tutorial: functions, modules, classes and environments](https://docs.python.org/3/tutorial/index.html) | `py-tutorial` / [Python tutorial: functions, modules, classes and environments](https://docs.python.org/3/tutorial/index.html) / supporting / 1.5 h |
| [Get started](https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/getting-started.html) | First test, assertions and test discovery |
| [Writing pyproject.toml](https://packaging.python.org/en/latest/guides/writing-pyproject-toml/) | Build backend, project metadata, dependencies and scripts |
| [copy](https://docs.python.org/3/library/copy.html) | Shallow and deep copy |
| [typing](https://docs.python.org/3/library/typing.html) | Type aliases, callable annotations and Protocol |
| [dataclasses](https://docs.python.org/3/library/dataclasses.html) | dataclass and default_factory |
| [contextlib](https://docs.python.org/3/library/contextlib.html) | contextmanager and cleanup |
| [asyncio tasks](https://docs.python.org/3/library/asyncio-task.html) | Coroutines, tasks, TaskGroup, cancellation and blocking calls |
| [threading](https://docs.python.org/3/library/threading.html) | Thread objects, locks and GIL/free-threaded notes |
| [multiprocessing](https://docs.python.org/3/library/multiprocessing.html) | Process, Pool, start methods and programming guidelines |
| [concurrent.futures](https://docs.python.org/3/library/concurrent.futures.html) | ThreadPoolExecutor, ProcessPoolExecutor, exceptions and deadlocks |
| [Profilers](https://docs.python.org/3/library/profile.html) | cProfile and interpreting statistics |
| [tracemalloc](https://docs.python.org/3/library/tracemalloc.html) | Snapshots, statistics and traceback comparison |
| [logging](https://docs.python.org/3/library/logging.html) | Logger, levels and handlers |
| [Function definitions](https://docs.python.org/3/reference/compound_stmts.html) | Function definitions: decorators and closure behaviour |
| [Timing clocks](https://docs.python.org/3/library/time.html) | perf_counter, process_time and clock measurement boundaries |
| [More control flow](https://docs.python.org/3/tutorial/controlflow.html) | Defining functions; default arguments; lambda expressions; function annotations |
| [Modules](https://docs.python.org/3/tutorial/modules.html) | Modules, packages and import paths |
| [Errors and exceptions](https://docs.python.org/3/tutorial/errors.html) | Handling exceptions, raising and cleanup |
| [Classes](https://docs.python.org/3/tutorial/classes.html) | Scopes and namespaces; iterators; generators; generator expressions |
| [Virtual environments](https://docs.python.org/3/tutorial/venv.html) | Creating virtual environments and managing packages |
| [Fixtures](https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/how-to/fixtures.html) | Requesting fixtures; scope; yield teardown |

### phase-1 / 02-numpy-and-tensors.md

| Reading | Selected sections / scope |
|---|---|
| [Quickstart](https://numpy.org/doc/stable/user/quickstart.html) | Basics; basic operations; universal functions; indexing; shape manipulation |
| [Broadcasting](https://numpy.org/doc/stable/user/basics.broadcasting.html) | General broadcasting rules and memory-inefficient cases |
| [Copies and views](https://numpy.org/doc/stable/user/basics.copies.html) | View, copy, indexing and other operations |
| [Data types](https://numpy.org/doc/stable/user/basics.types.html) | Array types; fixed-size types; overflow; extended precision |
| [N-dimensional array](https://numpy.org/doc/stable/reference/arrays.ndarray.html) | Constructing arrays; internal memory layout; attributes; array methods |

### phase-1 / 03-essential-mathematics.md

| Reading | Selected sections / scope |
|---|---|
| [Linear algebra](https://d2l.ai/chapter_preliminaries/linear-algebra.html) | Scalars through norms; matrix products and transposes |
| [MIT 18.06 selected linear algebra lectures](https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/18-06-linear-algebra-spring-2010/resources/lecture-9-independence-basis-and-dimension/) | `math-mit` / [MIT 18.06 selected linear algebra lectures](https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/18-06-linear-algebra-spring-2010/resources/lecture-9-independence-basis-and-dimension/) / supporting / 1.5 h |
| [Softmax regression](https://d2l.ai/chapter_linear-classification/softmax-regression.html) | Classification, softmax and log-likelihood |

## Reviewed video segment tables

Each range below has an actual-audio evidence artifact and separate transcript/frame review. It may support only a narrow prerequisite. Read caveats before treating a range as a lesson.

<a id="source-v01"></a>
### V01 — James Powell: So you want to be a Python expert? / PyData Seattle 2017

Publisher: PyData. [Original source](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cKPlPJyQrt4).

| Actual-audio ASR range | Extracted frame times (not all reviewed) | Reviewed content | Limits / corrections |
|---|---|---|---|
| [47:22–49:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cKPlPJyQrt4&t=2842) | [2878.0, 2926.0] | Actual2842–2962s and manually viewed2878/2926s frames introduce decorator syntax and runtime creation of function objects. This range ends before a full decorator transformation example. | Useful function-object prerequisite only, not complete decorator or closure teaching. Historical restrictions on decorator expressions changed in Python3.9; use PEP614 correction. Frame-review method: one image per local vision request + independent Tesseract text check [Primary correction](https://peps.python.org/pep-0614/) |
| [49:22–51:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cKPlPJyQrt4&t=2962) | [2998, 3046] | Actual2962–3082s and two2998/3046s frames reviewed: functions are runtime objects with metadata/defaults/code objects and inspect-based source lookup. | Function-object prerequisite; not closures or decorator transformation. Bytecode properties are implementation/version-specific. inspect.getsource is not guaranteed for every builtin/dynamic function; source must be available. No claim that all other-language functions lack runtime metadata. Frame-review method: FFmpeg samples + independent manual visual review; no model vision inference |
| [51:22–53:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cKPlPJyQrt4&t=3082) | [3118, 3166] | Actual3082–3202s and3118/3166s frames reviewed: source/line introspection and motivation for timing repeated function calls. | Despite acquisition label, this range DOES NOT teach decorator transformation. Do not map to decorator completion. Generic time.time subtraction is not a robust timing recommendation; perf_counter and repeated controlled measurements remain required. Captions locate actual timer/decorator explanation later3456–3669s; not yet actual-audio inspected. Frame-review method: FFmpeg samples followed by independent manual visual review; no local vision inference |
| [1:14:02–1:16:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cKPlPJyQrt4&t=4442) | [4478.0, 4526.0] | Actual74:02–76:02 audio explains Python iteration through iter/next methods and StopIteration. The two inspected frames show a live Python code demonstration. This supports the iterator-protocol portion of M01; it does not establish the separate ownership/copy, packaging or concurrency requirements. | Partial concept evidence: iterator protocol and incremental computation. Tiny projected code is not independently verified line by line. Frames inspected manually as well as by the local model. Frame-review method: one image per local vision request + independent Tesseract text check |
| [1:17:40–1:19:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cKPlPJyQrt4&t=4660) | [4696.0, 4744.0] | Actual1:17:40–1:19:40 explains yield-driven iteration, retained generator state and producing values on demand rather than eagerly storing a result list. Both manually viewed frames show the generator/iterator code comparison. | Useful generator intuition. The spoken no-storage shorthand is not zero memory: generator state and referenced objects persist. Exhaustion, exceptions, close/send and memory measurements still require practice. Frame-review method: one image per local vision request + independent Tesseract text check |
| [1:25:30–1:27:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cKPlPJyQrt4&t=5130) | [5166.0, 5214.0] | Actual1:25:30–1:27:30 motivates paired setup/teardown with files and introduces a SQLite example; both manually viewed code frames show the connection/cursor example. | Useful context-manager motivation, not full enter/exit or exception semantics. SQLite Connection context management commits/rolls back transactions; it does not close the connection. Do not transfer file-closing behavior to SQLite. File close/flush also does not by itself guarantee durable storage after power failure. Frame-review method: one image per local vision request + independent Tesseract text check [Primary correction](https://docs.python.org/3/library/sqlite3.html#how-to-use-the-connection-context-manager) |
| [1:30:10–1:32:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cKPlPJyQrt4&t=5410) | [5446.0, 5494.0] | Actual5410–5530s audio and5446/5494s code frames reviewed: a temporary-table context-manager class pairs enter/create with exit/drop and repeats the normal-exit demonstration. | Normal-path protocol illustration only. __exit__ receives exception type/value/traceback; truthy return suppresses exceptions. This range does not test exception cleanup, transaction safety or every resource lifetime edge case. Frame-review method: FFmpeg sampled frames independently manually reviewed; no vision-model inference |

<a id="source-v02"></a>
### V02 — Raymond Hettinger, Keynote on Concurrency, PyBay 2017

Publisher: SF Python. [Original source](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9zinZmE3Ogk).

| Actual-audio ASR range | Extracted frame times (not all reviewed) | Reviewed content | Limits / corrections |
|---|---|---|---|
| [9:55–11:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9zinZmE3Ogk&t=595) | [631.0, 679.0] | Actual9:55–11:55 discusses the historical GIL/Gilectomy trade-off and synchronization overhead. Both independently viewed frames show the speaker, not explanatory code or a benchmark. Its early free-threading performance anecdote is historical, not a measurement of modern free-threaded CPython or this machine. | Relevant historical concurrency context only. Pair with current official free-threading/threading documentation and record the actual Python build. This sample does not establish asyncio/process-pool behavior, safe shared-state code or current performance. Frame-review method: one image per local vision request + independent Tesseract text check |
| [17:34–19:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9zinZmE3Ogk&t=1054) | [1090.0, 1138.0] | Actual1054–1174s and1090/1138s frames reviewed. This is historical anecdotes about Twisted/Tornado/asyncio interest; frames show speaker, no API/code. | NOT async/await teaching; no AsyncIO concept coverage promoted. Prefer current asyncio documentation while seeking an instructional alternative. Frame-review method: FFmpeg samples with independent manual visual review; no vision inference |
| [22:36–24:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9zinZmE3Ogk&t=1356) | [1392.0, 1440.0] | Actual1356–1476s transcript and1440s frame reviewed: critical-section coordination and difficulty of proving large lock-based programs free of deadlock/starvation. Frame shows speaker, not code. | Historical conceptual synchronization introduction only. Acquire/release blocks other participants on that same lock; it does not globally stop threads or protect accesses that ignore the protocol. No concrete current lock implementation/test or complete maintenance argument. Frame-review method: One extracted frame independently manually reviewed; no vision-model inference [Primary correction](https://docs.python.org/3.13/library/threading.html#lock-objects) |
| [40:46–42:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9zinZmE3Ogk&t=2446) | [2482.0, 2530.0] | Actual2446–2566s and2482/2530s frames reviewed: introducing a worker Thread target/start instead of direct invocation; terminal demo begins. | Introductory historical code only. One successful run is not proof of concurrency correctness; race conditions and synchronization remain. A single worker conversion does not establish a safe multithreaded program or modern free-threaded performance. Frame-review method: FFmpeg samples with independent manual visual review; no vision inference |
| [43:04–45:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9zinZmE3Ogk&t=2584) | [2620.0, 2668.0] | Actual2584–2704s reviewed with2620/2668s podium frames: read-modify-write lost-update interleaving and scheduling-dependent bugs explain why repeated passing runs do not prove thread safety. | Historical Python2.7/3.6 printing and scheduling claims are not current-interpreter guarantees. The sampled frames show the speaker, not legible code. Synchronization repair is outside this excerpt. Frame-review method: FFmpeg sampled frames independently manually reviewed; no vision-model inference |

### V03 — Raymond Hettinger - Dataclasses:  The code generator to end all code generators - PyCon 2018

Publisher: PyCon 2018. [Original source](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=T-TwcmT6Rcw).

| Actual-audio ASR range | Extracted frame times (not all reviewed) | Reviewed content | Limits / corrections |
|---|---|---|---|
| [12:00–14:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=T-TwcmT6Rcw&t=720) | [756, 804] | Actual720–840s and756/804s frames reviewed: annotation syntax and third-party static checking are introduced before dataclass history/goals. | Short syntax/history introduction only; not Protocol or full typing practice. Type annotations do not automatically enforce runtime value types; do not equate this with annotation expressions never evaluating. ASR mangles attrs and historical names; those details are withheld. Much of range is historical commentary rather than instruction. Frame-review method: FFmpeg samples plus independent manual review; no model vision inference [Primary correction](https://docs.python.org/3/library/typing.html) |
| [34:43–36:43](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=T-TwcmT6Rcw&t=2083) | [2119.0, 2167.0] | Actual34:43–36:43 audio and visible slides discuss custom methods, field display/comparison/hash controls and metadata. The historical slide says a default_factory field is presumed excluded from hashing. Current Python3.12.13 counterexample instead raises TypeError for a list field with unsafe_hash=True: explicitly review current hash/compare rules. Current slots and kw_only options also postdate this2018 talk. | Useful historical explanation with an explicit current-version correction. This is not a blanket endorsement of all statements in the lecture. Dataclass code behavior was checked in a tiny separate local example; learner exercises remain unassessed. Frame-review method: one image per local vision request + independent Tesseract text check |

Publisher: SciPy. [Original source](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NoPY4aUJxwA).

| Actual-audio ASR range | Extracted frame times (not all reviewed) | Reviewed content | Limits / corrections |
|---|---|---|---|

This is not a proof of universal linspace accuracy. Choose domain-appropriate absolute/relative tolerances; no complete conditioning/stability theory or integer-overflow lesson. Frame-review method: Frame1 independently reviewed; frame0 not independently reviewed; sparse samples, no vision-model inference |
| [1:50:20–1:52:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NoPY4aUJxwA&t=6620) | [6656.0, 6704.0] | Actual1:50:20–1:52:20 introduces homogeneous ndarray dtype and shows inferred integer dtype. Manually inspected frames show np.arange(10) and dtype int64 on the demonstration machine. Speech self-corrects eight bit to eight byte/64bit; do not retain the initial slip as a fact. | Useful dtype introduction, recovered from the earlier setup-only sample. | [2:24:20–2:26:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NoPY4aUJxwA&t=8660) | [8696.0, 8744.0] | Actual8660–8780s ASR and8744s notebook frame reviewed: shape tuple, dimension count and reshaping24elements from1D to6x4; frame also shows a2x3x4 alternative. | This contiguous example changes metadata; not all requested reshapes are zero-copy. Current NumPy deprecates assigning arr.shape; prefer reshape and check sharing/copy requirements. Tuple attributes are replaced, not mutated in place. Frame-review method: One extracted frame independently manually reviewed; no vision-model inference [Primary correction](https://numpy.org/doc/stable/reference/generated/numpy.ndarray.shape.html) |
| [2:47:32–2:49:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NoPY4aUJxwA&t=10052) | [10088.0, 10136.0] | Actual10052–10172s ASR and10136s diagram reviewed:3x3int64 array with byte strides24 and8, row/column moves and dtype-size dependence. | These values apply to the shown contiguous shape/dtype. Views can have different, negative or zero strides; shape changes need not be zero-copy. NumPy byte strides differ from PyTorch element strides. Frame-review method: One extracted frame independently manually reviewed; no vision-model inference [Primary correction](https://numpy.org/doc/stable/reference/generated/numpy.ndarray.strides.html) |
| [4:03:20–4:05:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NoPY4aUJxwA&t=14600) | [14636.0, 14684.0] | Actual14600–14720s ASR plus14636/14684s frames reviewed: adjacent shifted slices and subtraction produce successive differences. Both frames show the wind-data plot; the exact difference expression is explained in speech but not independently visible there. | Input basic slices can share the original buffer; subtraction normally allocates its result. No local speedup or zero-total-allocation claim. ASR self-corrects1colon0 to1colon; use correct slice syntax and compare with a bounded loop before timing. Frame-review method: Both extracted frames independently manually reviewed; no vision-model inference [Primary correction](https://numpy.org/doc/stable/reference/generated/numpy.subtract.html) |

Publisher: PyCon South Africa. [Original source](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xGGVGo97fwo).

| Actual-audio ASR range | Extracted frame times (not all reviewed) | Reviewed content | Limits / corrections |
|---|---|---|---|
| [11:10–13:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xGGVGo97fwo&t=670) | [706.0, 754.0] | Not recommended as the primary reference-counting explanation: the reference-arrow diagram is useful, but the narration incorrectly conflates reference-count deallocation with a heap-sweeping cycle collector. | Ordinary GIL-enabled CPython generally deallocates on zero references, rather than awaiting a general heap sweep. Language-level timing is not guaranteed; finalization, cycles, immortal objects and free-threaded/deferred counting complicate it. A variable binding need not create an object or set its count to1. No concept mapping awarded from this flawed explanation. Frame-review method: Frame1 independently reviewed; frame0 not independently reviewed; sparse samples, no vision-model inference |
| [16:05–18:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xGGVGo97fwo&t=965) | [1001.0, 1049.0] | Rejected as a primary teaching recommendation because the collection criterion is misstated. | Do not recommend this range as the GC explanation. A self-reference example is useful, but narration incorrectly says the generational collector removes objects because they are old. Reachability, not age alone, determines garbage; generations are collection heuristics. Cycles can span multiple objects. del removes a binding, not necessarily the object. Expert-source replacement selected; no concept coverage awarded. Frame-review method: Frame1 independently reviewed; frame0 not independently reviewed; sparse samples, no vision-model inference [Primary correction](https://docs.python.org/3.13/library/gc.html) |

### V63 — Benjamin Peterson: Garbage Collection in Python - PyCon 2014

Publisher: PyCon 2014. [Original source](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iHVs_HkjdmI).

| Actual-audio ASR range | Extracted frame times (not all reviewed) | Reviewed content | Limits / corrections |
|---|---|---|---|
| [2:40–4:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iHVs_HkjdmI&t=160) | [196.0, 244.0] | CPython expert explains increment/decrement reference counts and deallocation when counts reach zero, including a cascade when a dying object releases another object. | Historical2014 conceptual model for conventionalCPython. Cycles, finalizer resurrection, immortal objects and modern free-threaded/deferred accounting require separate limits; Python language semantics do not guarantee exact finalization timing. A binding does not always allocate a fresh object. Frame-review method: Frame1 independently reviewed; frame0 not independently reviewed; sparse samples, no vision-model inference |
| [6:00–8:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iHVs_HkjdmI&t=360) | [396.0, 444.0] | Explains why mutual cycles can retain positive reference counts after external references vanish, and distinguishes periodic cyclic collection from zero-count deallocation. | Historical conceptual simplification: GC tracks eligible containers and uses temporary bookkeeping to subtract internal references, not blindly mutating all live object reference counts or scanning every object on every collection. Exact generations/thresholds vary byCPython version/build. Reachable old objects are not garbage merely because of age. No manualGC tuning or disabling performed. Frame-review method: Frame1 independently reviewed; frame0 not independently reviewed; sparse samples, no vision-model inference [Primary correction](https://docs.python.org/3.13/library/gc.html) |

## Phase 2 serving component map

Use this map inside the guide; no separate readiness README is needed. Code references below were checked at **vLLM v0.31.0**. Latest documentation may change; compare with the version you actually run. No server installation or phase-3 work is required to read these selected functions.

`request → API/input preparation → EngineCore → scheduler + KV ownership → executor/worker/model runner → output update → API response/stream`

| Component | Required reading target / boundary | Source |
|---|---|---|
| API/input boundary | Validate supported request/schema; apply tokenizer/chat template before engine ownership. OpenAI-compatible does not mean identical defaults or every feature. | [Selected source](https://docs.vllm.ai/en/latest/design/arch_overview/) |
| EngineCore | add_request validates request IDs/tasks and admits to scheduler; step schedules, executes, obtains/samples output, handles queued aborts, then updates scheduler output. | [Selected source](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/blob/v0.31.0/vllm/v1/engine/core.py) |
| Scheduler | Own waiting/running requests, token budgets and block admission; scheduling unifies token counts without making prefill/decode costs identical. | [Selected source](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/blob/v0.31.0/vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.py) |
| KV manager | allocate_slots assigns request slots; free returns ownership through the coordinator. Cache block release is not automatically physical CUDA allocator release. | [Selected source](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/blob/v0.31.0/vllm/v1/core/kv_cache_manager.py) |
| GPU worker/model runner | Worker delegates model loading and execution to a configured runner. Hardware/runners/backends vary; this is a code trace, not a local GPU execution result. | [Selected source](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/blob/v0.31.0/vllm/v1/worker/gpu_worker.py) |
| Cancellation/failure | Engine abort marks finished requests; in-flight block freeing may be deferred. A client disconnect is not proof immediate execution/physical-memory release occurred. | [Selected source](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/blob/v0.31.0/vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.py) |
| Health | At the reviewed v0.31 handler, engine death can return503; render-only mode can return200 without an engine. Liveness differs from representative-generation readiness and overload behavior. | [Selected source](https://docs.vllm.ai/en/v0.31.0/api/vllm/entrypoints/serve/instrumentator/health/) |
| Streaming/metrics | Check incremental client receipts, terminal/errors, metric units/labels and time boundaries separately. Lab snapshots are not HTTP streaming; server and client clocks differ. | [Selected source](https://docs.vllm.ai/en/latest/usage/metrics/) |

For E06/E08, explicitly list the missing HTTP, transport, queue, cancellation and health behavior. Do not claim those labs implement the complete serving stack. Trace the ordinary `step` path first; asynchronous/batch-queue alternatives need their own ownership/order checks.

## Practice and completion

Phase 1 requires 32 exercises and the repository assessment; Phase 2 requires 9 distinct experiments and its assessment. Reading/video review is preparation, not proof you can apply the material. Preserve observations, code and corrections, and retry independently after feedback.

## Video coverage limits and how to study

248/248 selected reading/practice paths have been reviewed at the pinned conceptual depth. 225/248 entries have partial inspected video support; 23 use the required-reading fallback shown in their row. For every entry: use the inspected segment for its stated scope, read the selected sections for uncovered details and corrections, then perform the linked check. Do not watch every full source video.

All 67 retained candidates have a disposition: 62 have inspected excerpts; failed/rejected sources remain explicitly unverified or rejected. No selected acquired clip is awaiting review. This is completion of this source audit, not a claim that every possible internet video was searched or that video coverage is complete.

The remaining reading fallbacks concern version-sensitive installation/serialization/allocator contracts, failed CUDA-event media, reproducible measurement and specific serving controls/APIs. They are explicit study requirements, not omitted concepts or fabricated video coverage. A related video may not teach the exact required contract.

Important corrections are retained beside segments: eval versus autograd; checkpoint-variable mismatch; preferred batch sizes versus allowed batch sizes; incompatible metric definitions; historical software behavior; GC reachability versus object age; and training memory versus inference memory. Some excerpts have one independently reviewed frame; this is stated per range. ASR text review is not human listening or full visual viewing.

## Maintenance

This is the single user-facing guide. Update verified rows and ranges in place; preserve user notes and assessment evidence. Supporting audit records are internal. Reopen the audit when adding new candidates or changing the curriculum; do not retain a completed status for unreviewed new scope. No cron, scheduler or new research platform is configured. Do not regenerate over user-added notes without preserving them.

## Assessment gates

[Phase1 rubric](https://github.com/noduxa/ai-inference-engineering/blob/0323221d26075752fcf59a18c5752e608786fc98/phase-1/assessments/rubric.md) and [Phase2 rubric](https://github.com/noduxa/ai-inference-engineering/blob/0323221d26075752fcf59a18c5752e608786fc98/phase-2/assessments/rubric.md) define completion. Independent explanation/application, diagnosis and fresh retries after assisted correction are required; missing evidence and critical misconceptions remain blockers.

Study block: predict/explain first → relevant video segment → read uncovered details → linked practice → closed-source teach-back → record mistakes and independently retry. Do not watch every linked full course.
